Millard2026-01-21 19:22 UTC
OK, confirm right now that whenever a REINIT flag (TexGVar (TV) object) is found, all lines for all detected indicators on the chart are being completely recopied and recalculated. If the recalculation is selective, tell me how the code figures out which one to fully recopy and recalc.
Claude2026-01-21 19:22 UTC

Confirmed: When ANY REINIT flag is found, ALL lines for ALL indicators are completely recopied and recalculated.

Trace:

  1. CheckReinitSignals() (lines 582-625) iterates through g_lines, and upon finding ANY REINIT flag for any indicator, it deletes that flag and returns true immediately (line 621).

  2. In OnTimer() (lines 903-933), when needsRescan is true:

    • ALL handles in g_lines are released (lines 920-924)
    • g_lines is completely cleared: ArrayResize(g_lines, 0) (line 925)
    • ScanChartIndicators() rescans ALL indicators (line 928)
    • ChartSetSymbolPeriod() forces full recalculation (line 931)

  3. In ScanChartIndicators(), every new line entry gets lastRatesTotal = 0 (line 521).

  4. In OnCalculate():

    • forceFullRecalc = true when lastRatesTotal == 0 (line 1072)
    • Full cache copy occurs when lastRates == 0 (lines 1086-1116)
    • startBar = oldestSlopeBar triggers full slope recalculation (lines 1173-1176)

No selectivity. A single REINIT flag from one indicator triggers complete teardown and rebuild of all tracked indicators.

Millard2026-01-21 19:28 UTC
I don't need explanations, just answers. Don't elaborate unless I ask. Go through the code and figure out what changes are needed to make it selective. DO NOT CODE until I give you a GO. All I want right now is a succinct plan for all the changes needed.
Claude2026-01-21 19:28 UTC

Plan:

  1. CheckReinitSignals() — Change return type from bool to return the specific instanceID and shortName that triggered (or -1/empty if none).

  2. New function: ReinitSingleIndicator(instanceID, shortName) —

    • Release handle for matching lines only
    • Remove only matching entries from g_lines
    • Re-discover and re-add lines for that single indicator
    • Set lastRatesTotal = 0 for those lines only

  3. OnTimer() —

    • Call selective reinit when CheckReinitSignals returns a specific indicator
    • Keep full rescan only for indicator count changes

  4. Remove ChartSetSymbolPeriod() call — Resetting lastRatesTotal on affected lines is sufficient; the blanket refresh is unnecessary.

Millard2026-01-21 19:34 UTC
so the only time we actually need to loop through all the lines and do full copies/recalcs for all of them is when prev_calculated = 0, otherwise we only calculate the current bar plus any new bars that came onto the chart. Correct?
Claude2026-01-21 19:35 UTC

Correct, with one nuance: it's per-line. Each line recalcs fully when prev_calculated == 0 OR when that line's lastRatesTotal == 0. Otherwise, only new bars + bar 0.

Millard2026-01-21 19:37 UTC
Yes, it's per-line. We have to go through all the lines on each tick, but after the initial run of OnCalculate, everythings populated and we just keep it current UNLESS a line's lastRatesTotal == 0, in which case we fully recopy/recalc. So, we don't need a separate bool, lastRatesTotal flags a line for full recalc. Right?
Claude2026-01-21 19:37 UTC

Right.

Millard2026-01-21 19:39 UTC
OK, take one more pass through the code with the plan in mind and find any potential problems, conflict, or omissions.
Claude2026-01-21 19:39 UTC

Problems identified:

  1. Buffer index management — Lines hold assigned bufferIndex values. On selective removal+re-add, need to either reuse freed indices or append new ones. Appending causes index drift over repeated reinits; reusing requires tracking freed indices.

  2. Stale buffer data — Removed lines' buffers must be cleared (set to EMPTY_VALUE) and plots set to DRAW_NONE, otherwise summary calculations will include stale values.

  3. g_totalBuffers / g_totalPlots — These globals assume contiguous assignment. Selective removal breaks that assumption. Need to either maintain "max index in use" or track active indices differently.

  4. Multiple simultaneous REINIT flags — Plan processes one per timer tick. If two indicators reinit simultaneously, second flag persists until next tick. Acceptable but worth noting.

Millard2026-01-21 19:43 UTC
You're being sloppy. The indicators we get handles for have buffers. IS has buffers. Restate the problems to make it explicit which you're referring to.
Claude2026-01-21 19:43 UTC

Problems restated:

  1. IS buffer index management — Each g_lines entry has a bufferIndex pointing to an IS output buffer (Buffer0-Buffer29). Selective removal+re-add: need to reuse freed IS buffer indices or append. Appending causes drift; reusing requires tracking freed IS buffer indices.

  2. Stale IS buffer data — Removed lines' IS output buffers must be cleared (EMPTY_VALUE) and their IS plots set to DRAW_NONE, otherwise IS summary calculations (Buffer30/31) will include stale slope values.

  3. g_totalBuffers / g_totalPlots — These track IS output buffer/plot allocation. Selective removal breaks contiguous assumption. Need either "max IS index in use" or track active IS indices differently.

  4. Multiple simultaneous REINIT flags — Plan processes one per timer tick. If two source indicators reinit simultaneously, second flag persists until next tick. Acceptable but worth noting.

Millard2026-01-21 19:47 UTC

"plot" refers to IS plots or SI lines/plots? 3. they would have to put the REINIT flags out there at exactly the same time, because the SIs clean up all existing flags before creating a new one. Claude2026-01-21 19:48 UTC

  1. Yes. The IS buffer holding that SI's slope data becomes stale.

  2. IS plots. Each IS buffer has a corresponding IS plot configuration. Without setting to DRAW_NONE, it could still render stale data.

  3. Acknowledged. Effectively impossible.

Millard2026-01-21 19:48 UTC

Claude2026-01-21 19:48 UTC

From the IS buffers. If the buffer is cleared to EMPTY_VALUE, the plot has nothing to render. Setting DRAW_NONE is redundant.

Millard2026-01-21 19:49 UTC

Claude2026-01-21 19:49 UTC

There are no plot variables. Plots are configured via indices (0-31) using PlotIndexSet* functions. The buffers are Buffer0 through Buffer31.

You're right to challenge. Looking again: they're incremented together (lines 548-549) and always equal in this code. It's either redundancy or anticipating a future case where calculation-only buffers (no plot) might be needed. Currently, they're effectively the same.

Millard2026-01-21 19:54 UTC

Claude2026-01-21 19:54 UTC

Yes. Without reusing freed IS buffer indices, repeated add/remove cycles eventually exhaust the 30 available slots.

Claude2026-01-21 19:55 UTC

  2. New ReinitSingleIndicator() — selective removal, clear affected IS buffers, re-add, set lastRatesTotal = 0

Millard2026-01-21 19:56 UTC
Explain in detailed sequential steps what #1 and #2 will do.
Claude2026-01-21 19:56 UTC

#1 CheckReinitSignals() changes:

  1. Iterate through g_lines looking for REINIT flags (same as now)
  2. When found, capture instanceID and shortName before deleting the flag
  3. Delete the REINIT flag (same as now)
  4. Return instanceID and shortName instead of bool (use struct or output parameters)
  5. If none found, return -1/empty

#2 ReinitSingleIndicator(instanceID, shortName):

  1. Loop through g_lines backwards
  2. For each entry matching instanceID+shortName:
    • Release its indicator handle
    • Clear its IS buffer to EMPTY_VALUE
    • Set its IS plot to DRAW_NONE
    • Remove entry from g_lines array
  3. Find the SI window via FindIndicatorWindow(shortName)
  4. Get fresh handle via ChartIndicatorGet()
  5. Get plot count from TextGVar
  6. Get maxPeriod from TextGVar
  7. For each plot:
    • Reuse lowest freed IS buffer index (or append if none freed)
    • Create new g_lines entry with lastRatesTotal = 0
    • Configure IS buffer and plot properties
  8. ChartRedraw()
Millard2026-01-21 19:57 UTC
#1.2 - which instance and shortname are captured?
Claude2026-01-21 19:58 UTC

The instanceID and shortName from g_lines[i] — the OLD values stored in IS from when it originally discovered that SI.

Millard2026-01-21 19:59 UTC
OK, so when an SI changes, the REINIT points to its previous parameters, and the TV metadata points to its newly changed parameters (new InstanceID). Agreed?
Claude2026-01-21 19:59 UTC

Agreed.

Millard2026-01-21 20:01 UTC
You're going to do 2.2 whether the SI was removed from the chart or it's parameters were changed, same procedure both ways?
Claude2026-01-21 20:01 UTC

No. Need to distinguish:

  • SI removed from chart: Cleanup only (steps 2.1-2.4). FindIndicatorWindow() returns -1, stop there.
  • SI parameters changed: Cleanup (steps 2.1-2.4), then re-add (steps 2.5-2.10).

Step 2.3 (FindIndicatorWindow) determines which path.

Millard2026-01-21 20:02 UTC
You basically said "No" and then explained how the answer was partially "Yes". Clearly list the two sets of steps for each situation, first when SI removed, second when parameters updated.
Claude2026-01-21 20:03 UTC

SI removed from chart:

  1. Loop g_lines backwards for entries matching old instanceID+shortName
  2. Release indicator handle
  3. Clear IS buffer to EMPTY_VALUE
  4. Set IS plot to DRAW_NONE
  5. Remove entry from g_lines

SI parameters changed:

  1. Loop g_lines backwards for entries matching old instanceID+shortName
  2. Release indicator handle
  3. Clear IS buffer to EMPTY_VALUE
  4. Set IS plot to DRAW_NONE
  5. Remove entry from g_lines
  6. Discover new SI metadata (new instanceID, new shortName) by searching TV objects for matching indicator base name (e.g., "ATR(") not already in g_lines
  7. Find SI window via FindIndicatorWindow(new shortName)
  8. Get fresh handle via ChartIndicatorGet()
  9. Get plot count from TextGVar
  10. Get maxPeriod from TextGVar
  11. For each plot: create g_lines entry with lastRatesTotal = 0, configure IS buffer/plot
  12. ChartRedraw()
Millard2026-01-21 20:05 UTC
on change: why are 4 and 5 necessary? We know we're going to redraw it, so why DRAW_NONE when we'd just have to reverse it anyway, and why break continuity when g_lines actually refers to exactly the same line?
Claude2026-01-21 20:05 UTC

You're right. For parameter change:

  1. Find g_lines entry matching old instanceID+shortName
  2. Release old handle
  3. Discover new SI metadata (new instanceID, new shortName)
  4. Find SI window, get fresh handle
  5. Update g_lines entry in place: new instanceID, new shortName, new handle, new period, new cached TV values
  6. Set lastRatesTotal = 0
  7. Clear IS buffer to EMPTY_VALUE (forces clean recalc)
Millard2026-01-21 20:06 UTC

Waiting.

Millard2026-01-21 20:07 UTC
when IS finds a REINIT flag and saves the old InstanceID and old shortname, how does it connect those to the same SI that threw them and identify its new InstanceID and shortname?
Claude2026-01-21 20:08 UTC

It can't directly. The REINIT flag contains old identity; the new TextGVar objects have a new instanceID and potentially new shortName (e.g., "ATR(14)" → "ATR(20)").

Possible connections:

  1. Same base indicator name (e.g., "ATR")
  2. Same chart window (stored in g_lines.indicatorWindow)

Problem: If two ATR indicators exist, ambiguous which one changed.

Millard2026-01-21 20:08 UTC
I'm not asking a fucking theoretical question. LOOK AT THE CODE.
Claude2026-01-21 20:08 UTC

Looking at HandleIndicatorDeinit (lines 169-192):

The REINIT flag is created with the OLD instanceID and OLD shortName. Its text value is also the OLD shortName.

After SI's OnInit runs, NEW TextGVar objects exist with a NEW instanceID and NEW shortName (e.g., "ATR(20)" instead of "ATR(14)").

Connection: Extract base indicator name (indyName) from old shortName (e.g., "ATR" from "ATR(14)"). Search TextGVar ShortName objects for one with same base name that has an instanceID NOT already in g_lines. That's the new one.

Millard2026-01-21 20:10 UTC

Claude2026-01-21 20:10 UTC

Correct.

Millard2026-01-21 20:11 UTC

Claude2026-01-21 20:11 UTC

IndicatorSlopes_MJM.mq5:

  1. Eliminate g_totalPlots. Replace all references with g_totalBuffers.

  2. Modify CheckReinitSignals():

    • Return old instanceID and old shortName (struct or output params)
    • Return -1/empty if no REINIT found

  3. New function ReinitSingleIndicator(oldInstanceID, oldShortName):

    • Extract base indicator name from old shortName
    • Search TV ShortName objects for same base name with instanceID not in g_lines
    • If not found → SI removed:
      1. Loop g_lines backwards for entries matching old instanceID+shortName
      2. Release indicator handle
      3. Clear IS buffer to EMPTY_VALUE
      4. Set IS plot to DRAW_NONE
      5. Remove entry from g_lines
    • If found → SI parameters changed:
      1. Find g_lines entry matching old instanceID+shortName
      2. Release old handle
      3. Get fresh handle via FindIndicatorWindow + ChartIndicatorGet using new shortName
      4. Update g_lines entry in place: new instanceID, new shortName, new handle, new period, new cached TV values
      5. Set lastRatesTotal = 0
      6. Clear IS buffer to EMPTY_VALUE

  4. Modify OnTimer():

    • Call CheckReinitSignals() to get old instanceID/shortName
    • If found, call ReinitSingleIndicator()
    • Keep full rescan only for indicator count changes

No changes to other files.

Millard2026-01-21 20:13 UTC
found → SI parameters changed #3 - where does it get the new shortname from?
Claude2026-01-21 20:13 UTC

From the TextGVar ShortName object found in the search step. The object's text value (OBJPROP_TEXT) contains the new shortName.

Millard2026-01-21 20:14 UTC
OK, restate "Search TV ShortName objects for same base name with instanceID not in g_lines" to make it more detailed and clear. (just that one bullet)
Claude2026-01-21 20:14 UTC

Search TV ShortName objects for the new SI:

  1. Loop through all chart objects
  2. Find objects matching pattern MJM_*_ShortName
  3. Parse each to extract instanceID and shortName
  4. Check if shortName starts with base name + "(" (e.g., "ATR(")
  5. Check if that instanceID does NOT exist in any g_lines entry
  6. Match found = new SI; its instanceID is the new instanceID, its text value is the new shortName
Millard2026-01-21 20:16 UTC
OK, that makes sense, but doesn't it need to be done differently in the case of a REINIT? In that case you have the old InstanceID and shortname, so the g_lines entry can be identified explicitly. Two different searches. Is that what your plan anticipates?
Claude2026-01-21 20:16 UTC

Yes. Two different searches:

  1. Find g_lines entry to update: Direct match on old instanceID + old shortName passed from CheckReinitSignals.

  2. Find new SI metadata: Process-of-elimination search through TV ShortName objects for same base name with instanceID not in g_lines.

Plan anticipates both but I stated them unclearly. Step 3.If found #1 is search 1; the "search TV ShortName objects" bullet is search 2.

Millard2026-01-21 20:17 UTC
OK, I agree, but restate the plan with enough detail and clarity to make things like that unambiguous.
Claude2026-01-21 20:17 UTC

IndicatorSlopes_MJM.mq5:

  1. Eliminate g_totalPlots. Replace all references with g_totalBuffers.

  2. Modify CheckReinitSignals():

    • Return old instanceID and old shortName (struct or output params)
    • Return -1/empty if no REINIT found

  3. New function ReinitSingleIndicator(oldInstanceID, oldShortName):

    Step A: Find g_lines entry using old identity

    • Loop through g_lines
    • Match entry where instanceID == oldInstanceID AND shortName == oldShortName
    • Store the array index (lineIdx)

    Step B: Extract base indicator name

    • Parse oldShortName to get base name (e.g., "ATR(14)" → "ATR")

    Step C: Search for new SI metadata

    • Loop through all chart objects
    • Find objects matching pattern MJM_*_ShortName
    • Parse each to extract instanceID and shortName
    • Check if shortName starts with base name + "("
    • Check if that instanceID does NOT exist in any g_lines entry
    • If match found: capture new instanceID and new shortName
    • If no match found: SI was removed

    Step D: If SI removed (no match in Step C):

    1. Release g_lines[lineIdx].indicatorHandle
    2. Clear IS buffer at g_lines[lineIdx].bufferIndex to EMPTY_VALUE
    3. Set IS plot at g_lines[lineIdx].bufferIndex to DRAW_NONE
    4. Remove g_lines[lineIdx] from array

    Step E: If SI parameters changed (match found in Step C):

    1. Release g_lines[lineIdx].indicatorHandle
    2. Find SI window via FindIndicatorWindow(new shortName)
    3. Get fresh handle via ChartIndicatorGet(0, window, new shortName)
    4. Read new period from TextGVar using new instanceID + new shortName
    5. Read new color/width from TextGVar using new instanceID + new shortName
    6. Update g_lines[lineIdx] in place:
      • instanceID = new instanceID
      • shortName = new shortName
      • indicatorHandle = new handle
      • indicatorWindow = new window
      • period = new period
      • cachedColorStr = new color string
      • cachedWidthStr = new width string
      • lastRatesTotal = 0
    7. Clear IS buffer at g_lines[lineIdx].bufferIndex to EMPTY_VALUE

  4. Modify OnTimer():

    • Call CheckReinitSignals() to get old instanceID/shortName
    • If valid (not -1/empty), call ReinitSingleIndicator(oldInstanceID, oldShortName)
    • Keep full rescan only for indicator count changes

No changes to other files.

Millard2026-01-21 20:42 UTC
what's this code intended to do? If you reference g_totalbuffers here it will cause an error, because the buffer count is the highest buffer index + 1. Or do I misunderstand?
Millard2026-01-21 20:43 UTC
what's this code intended to do? If you reference g_totalbuffers here it will cause an error, because the buffer count is the highest buffer index + 1. Or do I misunderstand? SetBufferByIndex(g_totalBuffers); PlotIndexSetInteger(g_totalBuffers, PLOT_DRAW_TYPE, DRAW_LINE); PlotIndexSetInteger(g_totalBuffers, PLOT_LINE_STYLE, lineStyle); PlotIndexSetInteger(g_totalBuffers, PLOT_LINE_WIDTH, lineWidth); PlotIndexSetInteger(g_totalBuffers, PLOT_LINE_COLOR, lineColor); PlotIndexSetString(g_totalBuffers, PLOT_LABEL, shortName + " " + lineName + " Slope");
Claude2026-01-21 20:43 UTC

You're correct that g_totalBuffers equals highest index + 1 AFTER the loop completes. But during configuration, g_totalBuffers is used BEFORE the increment:

  1. g_totalBuffers = 0
  2. Configure buffer/plot at index 0
  3. g_totalBuffers++ → now 1
  4. Configure buffer/plot at index 1
  5. g_totalBuffers++ → now 2

So at configuration time it's the current index, not the count. This is pre-existing code — I only replaced g_totalPlots with g_totalBuffers since they were identical.

Millard2026-01-21 20:43 UTC

Millard2026-01-21 20:50 UTC
The plan said to remove g_lines, for example when an indicator is removed from the chart. How is the code doing this?
Claude2026-01-21 20:50 UTC

In Step D of ReinitSingleIndicator (around line 1020-1025 in the new code):

//--- Remove entry from g_lines
for(int i = lineIdx; i < ArraySize(g_lines) - 1; i++)
{
   g_lines[i] = g_lines[i + 1];
}
ArrayResize(g_lines, ArraySize(g_lines) - 1);

Shifts all elements after lineIdx down by one, then shrinks the array.
